# How to choose between PRED750, H2KM, BD2K, PAS2400, and PAS5000
Product: Scrubber Selector
Category: Product Selection
Source: MonSuite Scrubber Selector (/scrubber-selector)
Keywords: scrubber selector, PRED750, H2KM, BD2K, PAS2400, PAS5000, which scrubber, residential, wet, metal, plastic
Answer: Use PRED750 for smaller/portable 750 CFM-class jobs. Use H2KM/H2KMA as the main metal 2,000 CFM workhorse for most commercial/industrial/heavy containment jobs. Use BD2K-XHP/XHPA when the same 2,000 CFM-class work benefits from a plastic cabinet, especially finished residential areas, scratch-sensitive spaces, or wet/damp restoration work. Use PAS2400 when 2,100 CFM and 120V power are needed with better mobility/stair handling. Use PAS5000 for very large containments where 4,000 CFM is useful and 230V/30A power is available.
Questions:
- Which scrubber should I choose?
- What are the main scrubbers?
- H2KM vs BD2K vs PAS2400
- Which scrubber for residential?
- Which scrubber for large containment?

# PAS5000 4,000 CFM large scrubber overview
Product: PAS5000
Category: Scrubber Selector
Source: PAS5000 Instruction Manual (/scrubber-selector)
Keywords: PAS5000, 4000 CFM, 230 VAC, 30 amp, 21 amps, large containment, galvanized steel, H242406-99, VL2024, H2080P
Answer: PAS5000 is the large real Abatement HEPA-AIRE portable air filtration unit for major containments. It is rated at 4,000 CFM with clean filters and requires 230 VAC, 60 Hz, single phase, 30A power; normal operation draws up to about 21A. It uses two H242406-99 HEPA filters, two H2002 pleated filters, optional H2001 polypad pre-filters, and optional VL2024 carbon filters. Use it only when the job has the access, power, and scale to justify a 332 lb high-airflow unit.
Questions:
- What is the PAS5000?
- How much airflow does PAS5000 have?
- What power does PAS5000 need?
- When should I use PAS5000?

# PAS2400 2,100 CFM portable air scrubber overview
Product: PAS2400
Category: Scrubber Selector
Source: PAS2400 Instruction Manual (/scrubber-selector)
Keywords: PAS2400, 2100 CFM, 700 CFM, 120 VAC, 15 amp, 12 amps, stair climber, stainless steel, H1980P, VL2002
Answer: PAS2400 is a real Abatement HEPA-AIRE portable air scrubber with 700 CFM low speed and 2,100 CFM high speed clean-filter airflow. It runs on 120 VAC/60 Hz with a minimum 15A circuit and draws 12A or less. It has a stainless steel two-module design, transport dolly, stair climber assembly, and an included H1980P 12-inch manifold that can attach to intake or exhaust. Use it when higher airflow is needed on 120V power and mobility/stairs matter.
Questions:
- What is the PAS2400?
- How much airflow does PAS2400 have?
- What power does PAS2400 need?
- Is PAS2400 good for stairs?

# BD2K-XHP / BD2K-XHPA plastic scrubber overview
Product: BD2K-XHP / BD2K-XHPA
Category: Scrubber Selector
Source: BD2K-XHP/BD2K-XHPA Instruction Manual (/scrubber-selector)
Keywords: BD2K, BD2K-XHP, BD2K-XHPA, Bulldog, 2100 CFM, 1400 CFM, plastic cabinet, residential, wet areas, H242406-99, VL2024
Answer: BD2K-XHP/BD2K-XHPA is the plastic-cabinet BULLDOG portable air filtration unit in the same general class as H2KM. MonSuite treats it as the preferred 2,000 CFM-class option for residential/finished spaces where metal could scratch surfaces, and for wet/damp restoration areas where plastic is preferred. Use H2KM metal everywhere else unless the job specifically benefits from the BD2K plastic cabinet.

- When should I choose BD2K?
- Is BD2K better for residential?
- Is BD2K plastic?
- BD2K vs H2KM

# H2KM / H2KMA 2,000 CFM metal scrubber overview
Product: H2KM / H2KMA
Category: Scrubber Selector
Source: H2KM/H2KMA Instruction Manual (/scrubber-selector)
Keywords: H2KM, H2KMA, 2000 CFM, 1300 CFM, metal cabinet, galvanized steel, H2001, H2002, H242406-99, VL2024
Answer: H2KM/H2KMA is a real Abatement HEPA-AIRE portable air filtration unit in the 2,000 CFM class. It is the metal-cabinet workhorse: 1,300 CFM low speed and 2,000 CFM high speed with clean filters, 120 VAC/15A power, 10A or less normal operating amps, and 20 ga galvanized steel cabinet construction. Use H2KM/H2KMA for most commercial, industrial, healthcare construction, abatement, and general containment jobs where a metal cabinet is appropriate. For finished residential spaces, scratch-sensitive areas, or wet/damp restoration work, choose BD2K plastic instead.
Questions:
- What is the H2KM?
- What is the H2KMA?
- How much airflow does H2KM have?
- When should I choose H2KM instead of BD2K?

# PRED750 electrical and ducting notes
Product: PRED750
Category: Setup
Source: PRED750 Instruction Manual (/scrubber-selector)
Keywords: PRED750, 120 VAC, 15 amp, 1.9 amps, 10 inch duct, ducting, GFCI, stackable
Answer: PRED750 is listed in MonSuite with 120 VAC, 60 Hz, 15A circuit requirements, normal operating amps of 1.9A or less, and integrated 10-inch nominal inlet and outlet connections. Use the shortest ducting possible, minimize bends, prefer rigid duct where practical, and keep flex duct taut because ducting and restrictions reduce airflow.

- What power does PRED750 need?
- What duct size is PRED750?
- How many amps does PRED750 draw?

# PRED750 filters and carbon option
Product: PRED750
Category: Filters
Source: PRED750 Instruction Manual (/scrubber-selector)
Keywords: PRED750, F621, H502, H161606-99, VL1002, VAPOR-LOCK, carbon, HEPA, VOC, odor
Answer: PRED750 supplied filter stages are F621 1-inch coarse pre-filter, H502 2-inch pleated pre-filter, and H161606-99 HEPA filter. For low concentrations of odors, vapors, gases, or VOC support, use the optional VL1002 VAPOR-LOCK carbon filter. HEPA captures particles; carbon is the option for odor/VOC support.

Source: PRED750 Instruction Manual (/scrubber-selector)

It uses staged pre-filtration plus HEPA filtration, has a variable-speed clean-filter airflow range of 200 to 750 CFM, and can be configured for negative pressure, recirculation, or positive pressure support when ducting and site setup are appropriate. # what causes pressure failure
Product: Pressure Guide
Category: Troubleshooting
Source: Pressure Monitoring Guide (/pressure-monitoring)
Keywords: pressure failure, alarm, doors, barriers, filters, hvac, tubing, ducts
Answer: Common pressure failure causes include doors left open, bad seals, torn barriers, clogged filters, undersized HEPA units, failed fans, blocked ducts, too much entry/exit traffic, HVAC imbalance, incorrect tubing setup, wind, or stack effect. Continuous monitoring matters because a manual reading only shows one moment.
Questions:
- Why did pressure fail?
- Why is my room not holding pressure?
- Why is the pressure alarm going off?
- What should I check when pressure is unstable?

# HEPA versus MERV filtration
Product: Pressure Guide
Category: Filtration
Source: Pressure Monitoring Guide (/pressure-monitoring)
Keywords: hepa, merv, filter, filtration, air scrubber, hvac
Answer: MERV ratings usually apply to building HVAC filters. HEPA is higher-efficiency filtration used for containment, isolation support, remediation, negative air machines, and portable air cleaning. A HEPA unit moves and filters air; a pressure monitor verifies whether the space is holding the correct pressure relationship.
Questions:
- What is the difference between HEPA and MERV?
- Does MERV mean HEPA?
- What filtration should I use for containment?

# basic ACH math
Product: Pressure Guide
Category: ACH Calculator
Source: Pressure Monitoring Guide (/pressure-monitoring)
Keywords: ach formula, cfm formula, room volume, calculate ach, calculate cfm
Answer: Room volume is length × width × height. ACH = (CFM × 60) / room volume. CFM needed = (target ACH × room volume) / 60. This estimates airflow only and does not prove pressure, containment, or compliance.
Questions:
- How do I calculate ACH?
- How do I calculate CFM needed?
- What is the ACH formula?
- How much CFM do I need for 12 ACH?

# ACH versus pressure
Product: Pressure Guide
Category: ACH
Source: Pressure Monitoring Guide (/pressure-monitoring)
Keywords: ach, air changes, cfm, pressure, ventilation, dilution
Answer: ACH means Air Changes per Hour. It describes how many times the air in a room is replaced or cleaned in one hour. ACH is about dilution and air cleaning. Pressure is about air direction. They are related, but they are not the same thing.
Questions:
- What is ACH?
- Is ACH the same as pressure?
- Can a room have ACH but fail pressure?
- Can negative pressure have poor ventilation?

# common pressure targets
Product: Pressure Guide
Category: Pressure Targets
Source: Pressure Monitoring Guide (/pressure-monitoring)
Keywords: pressure targets, pa, in w.g., -2.5 pa, -7.5 pa, -0.01, -0.02, -0.03
Answer: Common reference points include US airborne isolation around -2.5 Pa / -0.01 in. w.g., Canadian airborne isolation around -7.5 Pa / -0.03 in. w.g., and construction or abatement containment around -5 Pa / -0.02 in. w.g. Always confirm the actual target with the facility policy, project specification, consultant, and AHJ.
Questions:
- What pressure target should I use?
- What is a common isolation room pressure?
- What is a common construction containment pressure?
- What is -0.01 in water?

# positive pressure explanation
Product: Pressure Guide
Category: Positive Pressure
Source: Pressure Monitoring Guide (/pressure-monitoring)
Keywords: positive pressure, operating room, sterile storage, protective environment, clean space
Answer: Positive pressure means air is being pushed out of the protected space. It is used when the space itself needs protection from outside contamination, such as operating rooms, sterile storage, protective environments, clean rooms, pharmacies, and sensitive clinical spaces.
Questions:
- What is positive pressure?
- Why use positive pressure?
- What spaces need positive pressure?
- How does positive pressure protect a clean room?

# negative pressure explanation
Product: Pressure Guide
Category: Negative Pressure
Source: Pressure Monitoring Guide (/pressure-monitoring)
Keywords: negative pressure, isolation, containment, infection, dust, asbestos, mold, silica
Answer: Negative pressure means air is being pulled into a room or containment area. It is used when air inside the space may be dirty, infectious, dusty, or hazardous. The goal is to keep contaminated air from escaping into surrounding clean or occupied areas.
Questions:
- What is negative pressure?
- Why use negative pressure?
- How does negative pressure protect a hallway?
- What rooms need negative pressure?

# what pressure monitors do
Product: Pressure Guide
Category: Pressure Monitoring
Source: Pressure Monitoring Guide (/pressure-monitoring)
Keywords: pressure, monitoring, negative pressure, positive pressure, air direction, containment
Answer: Pressure monitors confirm whether air is moving in the intended direction between two spaces. They show whether a room or containment is negative or positive compared with a reference area. They do not clean air, filter air, or prove ACH by themselves.
Questions:
- What does a pressure monitor do?
- Does a pressure monitor clean air?
- Does pressure monitoring prove ACH?
- What is the purpose of pressure monitoring?

# zero/calibrate pressure reading safely
Product: RPM / PPM4 / Pressure Sensor
Category: Calibration
Source: RPM / PPM4 calibration guidance (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1fQubA6975M3K3Zy80_ylF3fbROo8CGRp/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: pressure, calibrate, calibration, zero, zero pressure, offset, sensor, do not blow, ports
Answer: When zeroing or calibrating pressure, remove applied pressure from the inputs and follow the monitor calibration workflow. Do not blow into hoses or pressure ports. If the reading will not settle, check tubing, filters, reference location, and whether the sensor is exposed to actual pressure before assuming the monitor is wrong.
Questions:
- How do I calibrate pressure?
- How do I zero pressure?
- Why is pressure not zero?
- How do I reset pressure reading?
- Can I blow into the pressure port?

# check Room and Reference pressure tubing
Product: RPM / PPM4 / Pressure Sensor
Category: Tubing
Source: Pressure Sensor Quick Guide (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1cOVjqPVg3SVflLI92JfK1A26HadMA2U1/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: pressure, tubing, hose, room, reference, ref, ports, differential pressure, negative pressure, sensor damage
Answer: Pressure readings depend on the Room and Reference tubing being connected correctly. Verify the Room line goes to the monitored space and the Reference line goes to the intended reference area. Check for reversed hoses, kinks, disconnections, blocked filters, or leaks. Do not blow into hoses or pressure ports because that can damage the sensor.
Questions:
- How do I connect pressure tubing?
- What are Room and Reference ports?
- Why is pressure reading wrong?
- How do I check pressure hoses?
- Can tubing be reversed?

# record pressure history and export data

Do not remove the USB drive until the download is complete. Downloading history does not necessarily erase internal records unless Delete Log is used.

# maintain required pressure
Product: RPM / PPM4
Category: Pressure Control
Source: Pressure monitoring best-practice guidance (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1cOVjqPVg3SVflLI92JfK1A26HadMA2U1/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: maintain pressure, keep pressure, negative pressure, positive pressure, stable pressure, containment, fan, airflow, leak, door, filter
Answer: To maintain required pressure, treat the monitor as the proof device and fix the room conditions causing the reading. Verify tubing and reference location, keep doors and containment openings controlled, check for leaks, confirm exhaust/negative-air equipment is running correctly, make sure filters are not clogged, and adjust airflow or containment before changing alarm limits.

- What if pressure keeps dropping?
- How do I hold pressure in a room?
- How do I maintain the required pressure?

# active pressure alarm troubleshooting
Product: RPM / PPM4
Category: Pressure Alarms
Source: RPM / PPM4 pressure alarm guidance (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1fQubA6975M3K3Zy80_ylF3fbROo8CGRp/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: pressure, alarm, active alarm, red screen, beeping, outside limit, threshold, troubleshooting, negative pressure
Answer: If a pressure alarm is active, first check whether the live pressure reading is outside the selected alarm limits. Then check Room/Reference tubing, reversed or disconnected hoses, blocked filters, doors/openings, containment leaks, fan or negative-air-machine changes, and whether the alarm points were set correctly. STOP ALARM silences the audible alert only; it does not fix the pressure condition.
Questions:
- Why is my pressure alarm going off?
- Why is the screen red?
- Why is the monitor alarming?
- What do I check when pressure alarm is active?
- How do I troubleshoot pressure alarms?

# PPM4 and RPM expansion bus capacity
Product: PPM4 / RPM
Category: Power
Source: PPM4 / RPM ()
Keywords: and, bus, capacity, charger, expansion, ma, power, power bus, ppm4, rpm, warning
Answer: PPM4 and RPM-S expansion bus capacity is modeled as 200mA. Both have two Ethernet ports. Sensors can daisy-chain, but the total load on a monitor-powered chain should stay at or below 200mA unless a Power Bus or local power accessory is used.

# dedicated power recommendation threshold
Product: Sensors
Category: Power
Source: Sensors ()

# what happens if bus power is exceeded
Product: PPM4 / RPM
Category: Power
Source: PPM4 / RPM ()
Keywords: bus, charger, exceeded, happens, ma, power, power bus, ppm4, rpm, warning, what
Answer: If the daisy chain exceeds the 200mA bus limit, the whole chain should be considered unsupported or unreliable. Do not assume only the last sensor fails. Possible symptoms include voltage drop, intermittent sensor detection, unstable readings, or devices randomly disconnecting.

# fix an overloaded sensor chain
Product: PPM4 / RPM
Category: Power
Source: PPM4 / RPM ()
Keywords: bus, chain, charger, fix, ma, overloaded, power, power bus, ppm4, rpm, sensor, warning
Answer: To fix an overloaded sensor chain, add a Power Bus, split the chain, or attach a PPM4 Charger to high-draw devices. Example: two Temp/RH sensors and one Pressure sensor draw 165mA. Adding ACH adds 100mA for 265mA total, which exceeds 200mA. If the ACH has local power, the bus load returns to 165mA.
Questions:
- fix an overloaded sensor chain?

# PPM4 Charger single device power logic
Product: PPM4CHRGR
Category: Power
Source: PPM4CHRGR ()
Keywords: bus, charger, device, logic, ma, power, power bus, ppm4, ppm4chrgr, single, warning
Answer: PPM4CHRGR is modeled as a local single-device power accessory. It powers only the one device it is attached to. If attached to an ACH sensor, that ACH sensor stays in the Ethernet communication chain but no longer counts against upstream bus power load.

# PPM4 battery conditioning
Product: PPM4
Category: Battery
Source: PPM4 Manual (https://docs.google.com/document/d/16K9WVfojveqaZWyiTfti14W0QO2rAuhv/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: battery, conditioning, manual, ppm4
Answer: Before first use, the PPM4 battery should be conditioned. Turn the power on and let it operate in AC mode until the battery discharges completely. Then charge until full charge is achieved. Use only the Abatement-supplied charger. Keep the battery charged every 6 months even if unused.

# PPM4 AC mode versus battery mode
Product: PPM4
Category: Operation
Source: PPM4 Manual (https://docs.google.com/document/d/16K9WVfojveqaZWyiTfti14W0QO2rAuhv/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: ac, battery, manual, mode, operation, ppm4, versus
Answer: PPM4 has AC Power Mode for continuous operation and Battery Mode for preserving battery life. In AC mode the monitor is active and the display times out while the LED stays on. In Battery Mode the display switches off after inactivity and the LED flashes periodically to show the unit remains operating.

# PPM4 firmware update warning

Keywords: firmware, from, legacy, ppm4, update, versions, warning
Answer: Critical MonSuite rule: if PPM4 firmware is below v1.8, update to v1.8 first before going to the latest v2.3 package. Do not jump directly from older firmware to the latest version unless engineering instructions explicitly say it is safe.

# PPM4 Wi-Fi validate fails
Product: PPM4 Wi-Fi
Category: Troubleshooting
Source: PPM4 Wi-Fi Troubleshooting (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1WZFT6sMLPnqtEJO-vOJjuNciZxRpv15q/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: fails, fi, ppm4, troubleshooting, validate, wi, wi-fi
Answer: If PPM4 connects to Wi-Fi but fails to validate, the issue can be caused by networks with advanced security measures such as hospitals or corporate environments. Try a simpler network/hotspot, confirm internet access, and escalate if validation still fails.
Questions:

# RPM do not connect to standard network
Product: RPM
Category: Connections
Source: RPM Manual (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1fQubA6975M3K3Zy80_ylF3fbROo8CGRp/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: connect, connections, do, manual, network, not, rpm, standard, to
Answer: Important: do not connect any RPM or sensor unit to a standard Ethernet network switch or router. The RJ45 connections are for the sensor bus, not normal Ethernet networking, and improper connection may damage RPM, sensors, or network equipment.

# RPM sensor setup DIP switches
Product: RPM
Category: Sensors
Source: RPM Manual (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1fQubA6975M3K3Zy80_ylF3fbROo8CGRp/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: dip, manual, rpm, sensor, sensors, setup, switches
Answer: Before connecting RPM sensors, plan the sensor layout. Each external sensor must be assigned a room and sensor number using DIP switches. Room 1 Sensor 1 is reserved for the built-in pressure sensor and cannot be assigned to an external sensor.

# RPM connect sensors
Product: RPM
Category: Connections
Source: RPM Manual (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1fQubA6975M3K3Zy80_ylF3fbROo8CGRp/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: connect, connections, manual, rpm, sensors
Answer: Use straight-through Cat5e or higher Ethernet cable for RPM sensor connections. Connect from either RJ45 port on the RPM to either RJ45 port on the sensor. Multiple sensors can be daisy-chained sensor-to-sensor. Maximum total cable distance from RPM to the last sensor should not exceed 50 ft.

Keywords: cellular, enable, guide, module, on, ppm4, quick, setup
Answer: On PPM4, enable cellular through Settings → Communications → Cellular. Then set the APN according to the carrier. If connection fails, verify plan type, LTE-M support, SIM activation, and APN.

# PPM4 Manual reference chunk 2: Note that this instrument is operated from an internal
Product: PPM4
Category: Document Reference
Source: PPM4 Manual (https://docs.google.com/document/d/16K9WVfojveqaZWyiTfti14W0QO2rAuhv/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: abatement, achieved, another, approximately, battery, before, charge, charged, charger, chunk, completely, conditioned, damage, depleted, discharges, document, even, every, from, full, fully, hours, instrument, internal, manual, note, operated, ppm4, reference, that, this
Answer: Note that this instrument is operated from an internal Lithium Polymer battery. This is a rechargeable battery which must be conditioned before use. When receiving the unit, turn the power on using the ON/OFF switch and leave it to operate in AC mode until the battery discharges completely. The screen and the LED will go off when the battery is depleted. The unit should then be charged until a full charge is achieved (approximately 7 hours). Note: Only use the Abatement supplied battery charger (MSS235). Use of another power supply can damage the unit. The unit is now ready for use. Please note that the battery should be fully charged every 6 months even if the instrument is not used.

# PPM4 Manual reference chunk 3: The PPM4 pressure monitor operates in two different modes
Product: PPM4
Category: Document Reference
Source: PPM4 Manual (https://docs.google.com/document/d/16K9WVfojveqaZWyiTfti14W0QO2rAuhv/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: able, active, after, always, approximately, automatically, battery, charger, chosen, chunk, continuous, continuously, designed, different, differing, display, document, from, hours, inactivity, indicator, installed, manual, modes, monitor, operates, ppm4, pressure, reference, the, two
Answer: The PPM4 pressure monitor operates in two different modes to satisfy differing requirements from the operator. These modes are the Battery Mode and the AC Power Mode. The AC Power Mode is designed for continuous operation when the operator wishes to be able to view the display at all times. In this mode, the display screen is always active and the LED indicator is ON continuously. This mode is optimally used with the Battery Charger installed and powered. If this Battery Mode is chosen with the on-screen menu and the unit is operated only from the battery, battery life is limited to approximately 8 hours. The Battery Mode is designed to preserve battery life for unattended operation. In this mode the display is automatically switched off after one minute of user inactivity. In this mode, the LED on the front of the unit will flash every 5 seconds to indicate that the unit is still operating and has not been turned off. The display will only re-appear in the event of an alarm event or if the screen is touched by the user. After touching the screen or pressing the power button, the display will appear but will switch off again after one minute. In Battery Mode, the unit will monitor pressures for up to 3days from a fully charged battery. Both modes are explained in detail later in this document. Note that pressure monitoring and Alarm Annunciation is identical in both modes. In addition, all events are saved in the internal archive in exactly the same manner for both modes. The difference between the modes is only in the appearance of the display.

# PPM4 Manual reference chunk 4: The AC or Battery modes are selected through the
Product: PPM4
Category: Document Reference
Source: PPM4 Manual (https://docs.google.com/document/d/16K9WVfojveqaZWyiTfti14W0QO2rAuhv/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: administration, are, attached, battery, behavior, button, case, chunk, connected, document, finally, first, holding, hose, icon, input, knows, labeled, life, lost, manual, maximized, menu, mode, modes, ppm4, reference, selected, the, through
Answer: The AC or Battery modes are selected through the administration menu. Note that, if the unit is set in Battery Mode and the power supply is attached, the icon will still show as Battery Mode. In this case, the TFT screen stays ON at all times since the unit knows that it has a source of power. If power is lost, the screen will revert to its normal Battery Mode behavior. Finally, when the unit is turned off by holding the ON/OFF button, the LED will remain off at all times. In this mode, battery life is maximized when it is not necessary for the unit to record and monitor pressures. There are two pressure sensor input ports on the pressure monitor. The first input port, labeled REF, is used as a reference pressure. The other end of the hose connected to this port must be in an area outside the space being monitored. If the pressure monitor is located outside the room being monitored, no hose needs to be connected to the REFERENCE input. The other end of the hose from the room being monitored is connected to the input port labeled ROOM.

Answer: A USB connector is provided for downloading the history file and for uploading new versions of firmware. The USB connector is designed for USB flash drives only. This switch is used to activate the display by a short press of the button. To switch the main power ON and OFF the ON/OFF button will need to be held for approx. 3 seconds. The mode can be observed by viewing the indicator LED on the front of the unit. The LED is on when power is ON. This power jack is used to connect the battery charger to the unit. While the charger is connected, battery charging is automatic. The unit will operate indefinitely with the charger connected. The battery will charge whether the unit is switched on or not while the charger is connected and powered.

# PPM4 Manual reference chunk 6: To operate the unit after initial charging, connect the
Product: PPM4
Category: Document Reference
Source: PPM4 Manual (https://docs.google.com/document/d/16K9WVfojveqaZWyiTfti14W0QO2rAuhv/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: able, adaptor, advised, after, allow, base, battery, cable, case, charged, charger, charging, chunk, closed, connect, cover, desired, disconnects, display, document, explained, initial, manual, operate, ppm4, reference, the, unit
Answer: To operate the unit after initial charging, connect the 1/8” tubing to the ROOM and REF input ports using the supplied quick disconnects. The quick disconnects are supplied with a right-angled adaptor to allow operation with the cover closed. Slots are provided in the base of the case to allow the tubing, the power supply cable and the BMS cable to pass to the outside when closed lid operation is desired. To be able to keep the battery charged while operating, the Battery Charger cable must be inserted into the power jack. If the system is operating in Battery Mode, be advised that the display will turn off after 1 minute of inactivity (Battery Mode is explained later in this document). Operating in AC Power Mode will keep the display on at all times. The filters provided with the box should be placed in line with each input tube in a convenient location. The arrow on the filter must point towards the HHPM on both tubes. NOTE: Caution must be exercised when closing the lid to ensure that the tubes and cables are not pinched. They must pass through their allotted slots.

# PPM4 Manual reference chunk 7: The pressure monitor settings may be accessed using the
Product: PPM4
Category: Document Reference
Source: PPM4 Manual (https://docs.google.com/document/d/16K9WVfojveqaZWyiTfti14W0QO2rAuhv/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: access, accessed, alarm, alarms, appear, audible, back, bottom, button, buttons, chunk, comprised, condition, connected, corner, document, external, five, gear, icon, main, manual, may, menu, monitor, ppm4, pressure, reference, settings, the, using
Answer: The pressure monitor settings may be accessed using the menu system. The menu system is comprised of a main menu screen and several sub screens. The main menu (shown on the right) is accessed by pushing the settings (gear) icon in the upper right corner of the main screen. The title of the menu is shown at the top. There are five submenu buttons in this menu screen which can be pushed to access the respective submenu. The BACK button is used for navigating to the previous menu. In an alarm condition, the STOP ALARM button will appear at the bottom of the main menu screen. Pressing the STOP ALARM button will silence the audible alarm as well as any external alarms through the web or mobile app if connected. The main screen will remain red until the differential pressure is within the user specified limit. The EXIT button is used for returning to the main screen. A detailed description of the menu structure and the available settings are outlined later in this document. The Room Settings menu will allow you to change various settings for the monitored room.

# PPM4 Manual reference chunk 8: Calibrate after applying zero pressure to the inputs and
Product: PPM4

Answer: Calibrate after applying zero pressure to the inputs and waiting for the differential pressure to stabilize. The operator will be prompted to remove all pressure inputs and press a button to continue. The calibration will take place and will return to the zero calibration menu when completed. Set or change the room name. The room name appears on the Main Screen above the sensor readings. If you click this name, a menu will appear to change this name. The Sensor Settings sub menu allows the user to enable/disable sensors, enable/disable alarms, and set the alarm point values. Alarm point values sets the alarm limit of the acceptable pressure range. If the room type is negative, anything above this limit will cause the system to alarm. If the room type is positive anything below this limit will cause the system to alarm. Range: -9.960 Pa (-0.0400 inWC) ~ 9.960 Pa (0.0400 inWC). Factory default is set to 0.0000 inWC. The Preferences Menu screen is to set or change various interaction settings on the device.

# PPM4 Manual reference chunk 10: Note: These settings will need to be customized for
Product: PPM4
Category: Document Reference
Source: PPM4 Manual (https://docs.google.com/document/d/16K9WVfojveqaZWyiTfti14W0QO2rAuhv/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: allows, archive, changed, chunk, complete, connected, customized, destroy, device, display, document, does, download, downloaded, downloading, drive, editor, event, file, flash, for, format, from, manual, need, note, ppm4, reference, settings, these, will
Answer: Note: These settings will need to be customized for the specific requirements of the BMS system the device is connected to. The history archive menu allows the operator to interact with the log history. The history will display the job number which was set when the event was recorded. The job number can be changed at startup and through the menu system. The operator will be prompted to plug in a USB flash drive for downloading. Offloading the historical information from the PPM4 does not destroy any information stored internally. The download process will start and the operator will be returned to the previous menu when the download is complete. Do not remove the USB device until the download is complete. The history is downloaded in a CSV format and can be viewed using any text-based editor. To view the file in a text editor, open the file titled “ARCHIVE.CSV”. All pressures recorded in the file are displayed in inWC units. Use only the USB Flash Drive supplied with the unit for this function.

# PPM4 Manual reference chunk 11: This option allows the operator to erase all of
Product: PPM4
Category: Document Reference
Source: PPM4 Manual (https://docs.google.com/document/d/16K9WVfojveqaZWyiTfti14W0QO2rAuhv/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: added, adjust, all, allows, archive, archived, auto, based, battery, been, brightness, case, change, changing, chunk, clock, current, device, displaying, document, enable/disable, entry, erase, manual, operator, option, ppm4, reference, the, this
Answer: This option allows the operator to erase all of the previously archived messages in the history. A new entry will be added to the archive history stating that the history has been erased. Allows user to set timer for screen going into rest mode, auto lock settings to set timer for locking the device, adjust brightness of screen, change the size of the text displaying pressure, as well as changing the language used in the software. Enable/Disable Knock Sensor which wakes the system up when its case is physically knocked. Enable/Disable Battery Mode which allows the system to maintain battery life for a longer period of time. Sets the current time with selectable seconds, minutes, and hours. The time used is based on a 24 Hr clock with, for example, 3:00PM being 15:00 hours. Enable/disable the password prompt for access to the main menu. When the operator enables the password the pressure monitor will automatically bring the user to the ENTER NEW PASSWORD screen. They must enter the same password twice to set the new password. Disabled as factory default.

# PPM4 Manual reference chunk 13: Firstly, ensure the device is connected to WIFI or
Product: PPM4
Category: Document Reference
Source: PPM4 Manual (https://docs.google.com/document/d/16K9WVfojveqaZWyiTfti14W0QO2rAuhv/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)

Answer: Firstly, ensure the device is connected to WIFI or cellular, this can be done through the instructions in the respective menus. in the menu, input the login credentials, these are the same as the credentials used to sign into the web application. If credentials are correct, the system will give an acceptance message After approx. 2 minutes, the device will be moved to the Verified Devices section on the web application There are two levels of device viewing; 1) a free tier that will allow you to get alarms notifications when the device is in an alarm state, 2) a paid subscription tier that will allow you to receive live updates on a set time interval. This will allow you to view the sensor values of the PPM4 when the device is withing the alarm parameters. Create an alarm notification to email or SMS text, select the Add Alarm. From the device list select Add Notifications. In the Add Notification Setup fill in the information and settings on how the Alarm notification will be sent.

# PPM4 Manual reference chunk 14: Once the account and device are setup on the
Product: PPM4
Category: Document Reference
Source: PPM4 Manual (https://docs.google.com/document/d/16K9WVfojveqaZWyiTfti14W0QO2rAuhv/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: account, and, apple, are, at-connect, calibration, chunk, components, consists, consumable, contact, could, credential, damaging, delayed, device, devices, differential, document, download, dust, external, filter, filters, manual, once, ppm4, reference, setup, the
Answer: Once the account and device are setup on the web portal, the device can be viewed from a mobile app. Use APPLE STORE or PLAY STORE to download the AT-CONNECT app. Login with the same credential as the web portal and view the devices registered to the account. The following outlines instructions on how to replace consumable components. Contact the manufacturer for replacement information. There are no user serviceable parts internal to this unit. Maintenance consists only of Zero Calibration as recommended and replacement of external filters. The inline filters should be used at all times while the pressure monitor is in operation. This will prevent any dust or moisture from damaging the internal sensors. Using a filter not specified by the manufacturer could result in delayed or incorrect differential pressure readings. Filter replacement is recommended to be done annually for very dirty environments. In normal, clean indoor environments, the filters can be used for up to 5 years.

# PPM4 Manual reference chunk 15: To clean the TFT display use a soft, damp
Product: PPM4
Category: Document Reference
Source: PPM4 Manual (https://docs.google.com/document/d/16K9WVfojveqaZWyiTfti14W0QO2rAuhv/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: actions, additional, after, away, back, based, before, best, black, cables, cause, chain, chemical, chunk, clean, cleaners, cloth, confirm, connected, daisy, damage, damp, display, document, manual, ppm4, reference, soft, tft, the, use
Answer: To clean the TFT display use a soft, damp cloth to gently wipe away any dirt or oil. It is best to do this with the system turned off so that dirt and oil is easily identified on the black screen. Use a horizontal or vertical motion to wipe the display. After using the damp cloth, use a dry cloth to wipe away any traces of moisture that may be left on the display. Do not use chemical cleaners on the display. Such actions may cause permanent damage to the display. The back of the sensor has ethernet ports, it does not matter which port you use, and if you have additional sensors, you can daisy chain them using ethernet cables. Before enabling the sensor in the software, set the DIP switches on the sensor based on: After the sensor is connected, DIP switches are set, and the sensor is enabled in software, confirm that the sensor appears active in the room menu or sensor status screen.

# RPM Manual reference chunk 1: • Do not enable any sensor that is not
Product: RPM
Category: Document Reference
Source: RPM Manual (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1fQubA6975M3K3Zy80_ylF3fbROo8CGRp/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)

Answer: • Do not enable any sensor that is not physically connected, or the system will generate a communication error alarm. 7 • Try power cycling the affected sensors by unplugging and re-plugging their Ethernet cables. 7 • Some setups may require daisy-chaining left RJ45 to left RJ45 for proper detection. 7 This manual will outline the operation and functionality of the Abatement Technologies Room Pressure Monitor RPM in detail. Visual aids have been added for additional help in proper setup and installation as well as navigating the system’s interface. The RPM (Room Pressure Monitor) supports connection of up to 10 external sensors, with a maximum of 5 per room. The unit includes one built-in pressure sensor, which is automatically assigned to Room 1, Sensor 1. Important: Do not connect any RPM or sensor unit to a standard Ethernet network switch or router. This may damage both the RPM and network equipment.

# RPM Manual reference chunk 2: Before connecting any components or applying power, plan your
Product: RPM
Category: Document Reference
Source: RPM Manual (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1fQubA6975M3K3Zy80_ylF3fbROo8CGRp/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: additional, affected, alarm, any, applying, assigned, assignments, before, built-in, cable, cables, cannot, cat5e, chunk, communication, components, connected, connecting, cycling, daisy-chain, distance, document, each, manual, plan, power, reference, rpm, your
Answer: Before connecting any components or applying power, plan your sensor layout. Each sensor must be assigned a room (1 or 2) and a sensor number (1–2). Set these assignments using the DIP switches on each sensor: *Note: Room 1, Sensor 1 is reserved for the built-in pressure sensor and cannot be assigned to an external sensor. Use standard straight-through Ethernet cables (Cat5e or higher). Maximum total cable distance from the RPM to the last sensor should not exceed 50 feet. Daisy-chain additional sensors by connecting an Ethernet cable from one sensor to the next. Do not enable any sensor that is not physically connected, or the system will generate a communication error alarm. Try power cycling the affected sensors by unplugging and re-plugging their Ethernet cables. Some setups may require daisy-chaining left RJ45 to left RJ45 for proper detection.

# RPM Manual reference chunk 3: Once the unit is powered, connect the 1/8” tubing
Product: RPM
Category: Document Reference
Source: RPM Manual (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1fQubA6975M3K3Zy80_ylF3fbROo8CGRp/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)

Answer: Once the unit is powered, connect the 1/8” tubing to the ROOM and REF input ports using the supplied quick disconnects. The quick disconnects are supplied with a right-angled adaptor to allow operation with the cover closed. Slots are provided in the base of the case to allow the tubing, the power supply cable and the BMS cable to pass to the outside when closed lid operation is desired. The filters provided with the box should be placed in line with each input tube in a convenient location. The arrow on the filter must point towards the HHPM on both tubes. To change the JOB Number. Click the current Job Number in the upper left corner of the screen and an input menu will appear to change the job number.

# RPM Manual reference chunk 4: The pressure monitor settings may be accessed using the
Product: RPM
Category: Document Reference
Source: RPM Manual (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1fQubA6975M3K3Zy80_ylF3fbROo8CGRp/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: access, accessed, alarm, alarms, appear, audible, back, bottom, button, buttons, chunk, comprised, condition, connected, corner, document, external, five, gear, icon, main, manual, may, menu, monitor, pressure, reference, rpm, settings, the, using
Answer: The pressure monitor settings may be accessed using the menu system. The menu system is comprised of a main menu screen and several sub screens. The main menu (shown on the right) is accessed by pushing the settings (gear) icon in the upper right corner of the main screen. The title of the menu is shown at the top. There are five submenu buttons in this menu screen which can be pushed to access the respective submenu. The BACK button is used for navigating to the previous menu. In an alarm condition, the STOP ALARM button will appear at the bottom of the main menu screen. Pressing the STOP ALARM button will silence the audible alarm as well as any external alarms through the web or mobile app if connected. The main screen will remain red until the differential pressure is within the user specified limit. The EXIT button is used for returning to the main screen. A detailed description of the menu structure and the available settings are outlined later in this document. The Room Settings menu will allow you to change various settings for the monitored room.

# RPM Manual reference chunk 5: Calibrate after applying zero pressure to the inputs and
Product: RPM
Category: Document Reference
Source: RPM Manual (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1fQubA6975M3K3Zy80_ylF3fbROo8CGRp/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: above, acceptable, after, alarm, alarms, allows, and, anything, appears, applying, below, button, calibrate, calibration, cause, change, chunk, completed, continue, differential, document, enable/disable, inputs, manual, pressure, reference, rpm, the, zero
Answer: Calibrate after applying zero pressure to the inputs and waiting for the differential pressure to stabilize. The operator will be prompted to remove all pressure inputs and press a button to continue. The calibration will take place and will return to the zero calibration menu when completed. Set or change the room name. The room name appears on the Main Screen above the sensor readings The Sensor Settings sub menu allows the user to enable/disable sensors, enable/disable alarms, and set the alarm point values. Alarm point values sets the alarm limit of the acceptable pressure range. If the room type is negative, anything above this limit will cause the system to alarm. If the room type is positive anything below this limit will cause the system to alarm. Range: -9.960 Pa (-0.0400 inWC) ~ 9.960 Pa (0.0400 inWC). Factory default is set to 0.0000 inWC. The Preferences Menu screen is to set or change various interaction settings on the device.

# RPM Manual reference chunk 8: The operator will be prompted to plug in a
Product: RPM
Category: Document Reference
Source: RPM Manual (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1fQubA6975M3K3Zy80_ylF3fbROo8CGRp/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: added, allows, archive, archived, chunk, complete, destroy, device, displayed, document, does, download, downloaded, downloading, drive, editor, entry, erase, file, flash, format, from, manual, operator, plug, prompted, reference, rpm, the, will
Answer: The operator will be prompted to plug in a USB flash drive for downloading. Offloading the historical information from the PPM4 does not destroy any information stored internally. The download process will start and the operator will be returned to the previous menu when the download is complete. Do not remove the USB device until the download is complete. The history is downloaded in a CSV format and can be viewed using any text-based editor. To view the file in a text editor, open the file titled “ARCHIVE.CSV”. All pressures recorded in the file are displayed in inWC units. Use only the USB Flash Drive supplied with the unit for this function. This option allows the operator to erase all of the previously archived messages in the history. A new entry will be added to the archive history stating that the history has been erased. Allows user to set timer for screen going into rest mode, auto lock settings to set timer for locking the device, adjust brightness of screen, change the size of the text displaying pressure, as well as changing the language used in the software.

# RPM Manual reference chunk 12: There are no user serviceable parts internal to this
Product: RPM
Category: Document Reference
Source: RPM Manual (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1fQubA6975M3K3Zy80_ylF3fbROo8CGRp/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: annually, are, away, best, black, calibration, chunk, clean, cloth, consists, could, damaging, damp, delayed, differential, dirt, dirty, display, document, done, dust, easily, environments, internal, manual, parts, reference, rpm, serviceable, there, this, user
Answer: There are no user serviceable parts internal to this unit. Maintenance consists only of Zero Calibration as recommended and replacement of external filters. The inline filters should be used at all times while the pressure monitor is in operation. This will prevent any dust or moisture from damaging the internal sensors. Using a filter not specified by the manufacturer could result in delayed or incorrect differential pressure readings. Filter replacement is recommended to be done annually for very dirty environments. In normal, clean indoor environments, the filters can be used for up to 5 years. To clean the TFT display use a soft, damp cloth to gently wipe away any dirt or oil. It is best to do this with the system turned off so that dirt and oil is easily identified on the black screen. Use a horizontal or vertical motion to wipe the display. After using the damp cloth, use a dry cloth to wipe away any traces of moisture that may be left on the display. Do not use chemical cleaners on the display. Such actions may cause permanent damage to the display.

Answer: Abatement Technologies Inc. (ATI) provides in-house services for calibration and repair of all pressure monitors purchased from ATI. Calibrations are traceable to the National Institute of Standards & Technology (NIST). DAKKS: DEUTSCHE AKKREDITIERUNGSSTELLE calibration is also available. If you would like to recalibrate or recertify your pressure monitor, please call our customer service department at 800-634-9091(US) or 800-827-6443 (Canada) for scheduling. Typical turnaround time is approximately 3 weeks after receipt at ATI. Non-warranty repairs will not be made without customer approval and a purchase order to cover repair charges. All models of room differential pressure monitors sold by ATI are warranted for a period of 1 year against defects in materials and workmanship. # RPM Manual reference chunk 14: the product has not been subject to abuse, neglect,
Product: RPM
Category: Document Reference
Source: RPM Manual (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1fQubA6975M3K3Zy80_ylF3fbROo8CGRp/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: abuse, accident, advance, agencies, altered, anyone, applicable, authorized, been, charges, chunk, defect, destination, developed, document, examination, exceeding, except, from, furnished, goods, has, improper, manual, neglect, not, product, reference, rpm, subject, the
Answer: the product has not been subject to abuse, neglect, accident, incorrect power supply or installation wiring, improper installation/servicing or use in violation of instructions furnished by ATI; the product has not been repaired or altered by anyone except ATI or its authorized service agencies; examination by an authorized ATI service representative reveals a defect in materials or workmanship developed under normal installation, use and service; ATI is notified in advance and the product is returned to ATI. # Temperature & Humidity Sensor Quick Guide reference chunk 1: Readings display on the main screen once enabled and
Product: Temp/RH Sensor
Category: Document Reference
Source: Temperature & Humidity Sensor Quick Guide (https://docs.google.com/document/d/187dJ1pq8qR1vd6x7KAR6W52YVRUP3nwh/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: alarms, and, assigned, built-in, cable, cables, cannot, choose, chunk, cloud, configured, connect, daisy-chain, dip1, dip4, display, document, either, enabled, ethernet, external, from, guide, have, humidity, main, once, quick, readings, reference, screen, sensor, temp/rh, temperature, the
Answer: Readings display on the main screen once enabled and can be used for alarms and cloud reporting (if configured). Do NOT connect RPM/PPM4 sensors to a standard network switch/router (this is a sensor bus, not Ethernet networking). Plan which Room (1 or 2) and Sensor number you want this sensor to be assigned to. *Note: Room 1, Sensor 1 is reserved for the built-in pressure sensor and cannot be used by an external sensor. Recommended: Use Room 1, Sensor 2 (DIP4 OFF, DIP1 ON) unless you have a reason to choose otherwise. Connect an Ethernet cable from either RPM/PPM4 RJ45 port to either RJ45 port on the sensor. If you have multiple sensors, daisy-chain them sensor-to-sensor with Ethernet cables. From the main screen go to: Menu → Room Settings → Room 1 → Sensor Settings → Sensor Enable Tip: Do not enable a sensor that isn’t physically connected or you may get a communication error alarm. Important: Alarms only trigger if Room Status = Occupied Check: Menu → Room Settings → Room X → Room Status → Occupied

# Temperature & Humidity Sensor Quick Guide reference chunk 2: Some RPM/PPM4 peripheral sensors can receive power from the
Product: Temp/RH Sensor
Category: Document Reference
Source: Temperature & Humidity Sensor Quick Guide (https://docs.google.com/document/d/187dJ1pq8qR1vd6x7KAR6W52YVRUP3nwh/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: abatement, appropriate, available, because, best, built-in, can, capacity, cellular, chunk, connected, count, document, does, exceed, exceeds, expansion, external, from, guide, higher-current, however, humidity, important, including, peripheral, power, quick, receive, reference, rpm/ppm4, sensor, sensors, some, temp/rh, temperature, the
Answer: Some RPM/PPM4 peripheral sensors can receive power from the RPM/PPM4 expansion bus. However, the expansion bus has a maximum available capacity of 200 mA total for connected peripheral modules. The Cellular Module requires its own power supply because it exceeds the RPM/PPM4 expansion bus capacity. For best performance, Abatement Technologies recommends using the provided power supply for higher-current peripheral sensors, including: Temp/RH and external pressure sensors may be powered by the RPM/PPM4 expansion bus as long as the total connected peripheral load does not exceed 200 mA. Do not add an air velocity sensor, particle sensor, or cellular module to this setup without using the appropriate external power supply. Important: The built-in pressure sensor is internal to the RPM/PPM4 and does not count as an external sensor module on the expansion bus. To receive notifications of software and product updates you must register using this QR code.

# Pressure Sensor Quick Guide reference chunk 2: • Install the pressure sensor in the area or
Product: Pressure Sensor
Category: Document Reference
Source: Pressure Sensor Quick Guide (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1cOVjqPVg3SVflLI92JfK1A26HadMA2U1/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: access, according, affect, airflow, appears, area, areas, avoid, blocked, check, chunk, confirm, connected, connections, contact, correct, damage, direction, directly, disconnected, document, easy, guide, install, pressure, quick, reference, sensor, the
Answer: • Install the pressure sensor in the area or room you want to monitor. • Mount the sensor where it is protected from damage and easy to access for service. • Avoid placing it: o Where tubing can be pinched, blocked, or disconnected o Directly in front of supply vents or strong airflow o In wet areas or where water spray may contact the sensor o In locations where vibration or impact may affect the sensor If the pressure sensor uses pressure ports or tubing, make sure the tubing is securely connected and not kinked. Confirm the reference side and room side are connected according to your pressure monitoring setup. Confirm the tubing is connected to the correct port for the pressure direction you want to measure. Tip: If the pressure value appears reversed, check the tubing connections and confirm the room/reference ports are not swapped. From the main screen go to: Menu → Room Settings → Room 1 → Sensor Settings → Sensor Enable Tip: Do not enable a sensor that isn’t physically connected or you may get a communication error alarm.

Answer: Pressure alarm limits should match the required pressure condition for the room, such as negative pressure, positive pressure, or neutral pressure. • Return to the main screen and confirm pressure values appear. • Confirm the reading changes when the room pressure condition changes, such as when a door opens or airflow changes. • If cloud reporting is configured, confirm updates and alarms appear in your web portal/app. • Confirm DIP switches match the sensor slot you enabled. • Confirm all RJ45 connections are fully seated. • Try unplugging/replugging the sensor Ethernet cable. • If daisy-chained, ensure chaining is consistent. Try left-to-left RJ45 if needed. • Power-cycle the RPM/PPM4 if needed.

Source: ACH / Velocity Sensor Quick Guide (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1of_UXpkELLnhNzrOpBh5U6la_a6zu--D/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: ach, adds, air, airflow, alarming, arrow, being, built-in, cable, cannot, chunk, cloud, configured, connect, direction, display, document, either, element, ethernet, exposed, external, for, from, guide, helps, quick, readings, reference, rpm/ppm4, sensor, velocity, your
Answer: Adds air velocity readings to your RPM/PPM4 for display, alarming, and cloud reporting, if configured. Helps verify ventilation, airflow direction, and air movement levels where the sensor is installed. Do NOT connect RPM/PPM4 sensors to a standard network switch/router. This is a sensor bus, not Ethernet networking. Make sure the air velocity sensor is installed in a location where airflow can reach the sensing element properly. *Note: Room 1, Sensor 1 is reserved for the built-in pressure sensor and cannot be used by an external sensor. Connect an Ethernet cable from either RPM/PPM4 RJ45 port to either RJ45 port on the air velocity sensor. Position the sensor so the sensing element is exposed to the airflow being measured. If the sensor has an airflow direction arrow, probe opening, or sensing window, make sure it is oriented correctly. Too close to supply vents, exhaust grilles, or fans unless that airflow is the intended measurement point Tip: Do not enable a sensor that is not physically connected or you may get a communication error alarm.

# ACH / Velocity Sensor Quick Guide reference chunk 2: Set the desired alarm thresholds for the air velocity
Product: ACH / Velocity Sensor
Category: Document Reference
Source: ACH / Velocity Sensor Quick Guide (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1of_UXpkELLnhNzrOpBh5U6la_a6zu--D/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: abatement, ach, air, alarm, alarms, appear, available, avoid, because, best, capacity, cellular, chaining, chunk, close, cloud, configured, confirm, connected, consistent, daisy-chained, desired, document, doors, for, guide, quick, reference, sensor, set, the, thresholds, velocity
Answer: Set the desired alarm thresholds for the air velocity reading shown by your system. If cloud reporting is configured, confirm updates and alarms appear in your web portal or app. If daisy-chained, ensure chaining is consistent. Try left-to-left RJ45 if needed. Avoid placing the sensor too close to fans, vents, doors, or obstructions unless those are part of the intended measurement. Some RPM/PPM4 peripheral sensors can receive power from the RPM/PPM4 expansion bus. However, the expansion bus has a maximum available capacity of 200 mA total for connected peripheral modules. The Cellular Module requires its own power supply because it exceeds the RPM/PPM4 expansion bus capacity. For best performance, Abatement Technologies recommends using the provided power supply for higher-current peripheral sensors, including: Temp/RH and external pressure sensors may be powered by the RPM/PPM4 expansion bus as long as the total connected peripheral load does not exceed 200 mA.

# ACH / Velocity Sensor Quick Guide reference chunk 3: Do not add an air velocity sensor, particle sensor,

# Particle Sensor Quick Guide reference chunk 1: Adds particle readings to your RPM/PPM4 for display, alarming,
Product: Particle Sensor
Category: Document Reference
Source: Particle Sensor Quick Guide (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1_AchivrGBuIcV7eezeRG6kt35pCYD_Gi/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: adds, alarming, built-in, cable, cannot, choose, chunk, cloud, communication, configured, connect, connected, direction, display, document, either, enable, error, ethernet, external, for, from, grill, guide, particle, quick, readings, reference, rpm/ppm4, sensor, your
Answer: Adds particle readings to your RPM/PPM4 for display, alarming, and cloud reporting (if configured). Do NOT connect RPM/PPM4 sensors to a standard network switch/router (this is a sensor bus, not Ethernet networking). *Note: Room 1, Sensor 1 is reserved for the built-in pressure sensor and cannot be used by an external sensor. Recommended: Use Room 1, Sensor 2 unless you have a reason to choose otherwise. Connect an Ethernet cable from either RPM/PPM4 RJ45 port to either RJ45 port on the particle sensor. (If your particle sensor has an intake direction, grill, or sampling port, keep it unobstructed.) From the main screen go to: Menu → Room Settings → Room 1 → Sensor Settings → Sensor Enable Tip: Do not enable a sensor that isn’t physically connected or you may get a communication error alarm. Set the desired alarm thresholds for the particle reading(s) shown by your system. Important: Alarms only trigger if Room Status = Occupied Check: Menu → Room Settings → Room X → Room Status → Occupied Check sensor placement (near doors, vents, work zones, or cleaning activity can spike readings)

# Cellular Module Setup Quick Guide reference chunk 1: The modem uses a standard 3FF (micro-SIM) form factor.
Product: Cellular Module
Category: Document Reference
Source: Cellular Module Setup Quick Guide (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1tCA0fPUpyCAxweMY27g-f1R6prsBPAXw/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: 3ff, allows, american, area, attach, bands, bell, best, block, canada, canadian, cards, carrier, carriers, cat-m1, cellular, chunk, commands, consumer, cross-border, data, delivered, document, euicc/esim, factor, form, guide, micro-sim, modem, module, quick, reference, setup, standard, the, uses
Answer: The modem uses a standard 3FF (micro-SIM) form factor. In practice, most Canadian and American SIM cards from major carriers will work, as long as the plan is provisioned for LTE-M (Cat-M1) data and the carrier supports the modem’s bands in your area. What works best: IoT/M2M data plans from major carriers in Canada and the U.S. (Rogers, Bell, Telus; Verizon, AT&T, T-Mobile) that explicitly include LTE-M. APN: You’ll need the carrier’s APN. Insert SIM → power on → set APN via AT commands, then attach. Roaming: Cross-border roaming is possible if your plan allows LTE-M roaming; otherwise, use a local SIM. eUICC/eSIM: Supported if delivered as a 3FF profile/SIM by your carrier and provisioned for LTE-M. Locked/Special SIMs: Consumer phone SIMs sometimes block IoT use or LTE-M; if attach fails, ask your carrier to enable LTE-M on the line. Once you have an activated SIM, you will need to ensure it is the correct size of MICRO or 15mm x 12mm. If your SIM is too small, adapters are available for purchase. If the sim is too large, the is usually a casing which can be removed to make it MICRO.

# Cellular Module Setup Quick Guide reference chunk 2: You will need to access the black plate of
Product: Cellular Module
Category: Document Reference
Source: Cellular Module Setup Quick Guide (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1tCA0fPUpyCAxweMY27g-f1R6prsBPAXw/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: able, access, according, apns, black, cable, card, carrier, carriers, cellular, chain, check, chunk, closely, direction, document, does, ethernet, first, gently, guide, have, insert, inserted, module, need, plate, quick, reference, setup, the, will, you
Answer: You will need to access the black plate of your monarch-go module. Remove this black plate gently to reveal this metal plate for our SIM to be inserted. Gently slide the metal plate to unlock it. If you look very closely, you can see ‘lock’ and ‘open’ with which direction to push the plate Insert your SIM card into the slot. Slide the metal plate into locking position, the SIM card should not be able to move at all once locked. Plug the module into the PPM4 using the provided ethernet cable. Sides do not matter, where it is in the chain of sensors does not matter. Set the APN according to your carrier, if the first APN does not work, check to see if your carrier has others. Most carriers have multiple APNs.

# Cellular Module Setup Quick Guide reference chunk 3: The Monarch Gois a pre-certified cellular + GPS/GNSS modem
Product: Cellular Module
Category: Document Reference
Source: Cellular Module Setup Quick Guide (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1tCA0fPUpyCAxweMY27g-f1R6prsBPAXw/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: antennas, application, attaches, bridge, building, calculates, cat-m1, cellular, chunk, cloud, combines, commands, custom, data, device, document, embedded, enabled, enclosure, establishes, fixes, from, gois, gps/gnss, guide, modem, module, monarch, pre-certified, quick, reference, setup, the
Answer: The Monarch Gois a pre-certified cellular + GPS/GNSS modem packaged for quick prototyping and integration. It combines an LTE Cat-M1 radio (for low-power, wide-area data on Verizon’s network) with an integrated GNSS receiver and embedded antennas inside a small enclosure. The kit presents the modem over USB as a virtual COM port, so you can send standard AT commands from a terminal or your application without building a custom radio front end. Cellular link (LTE-M): Your device talks to the modem over UART (via the kit’s USB bridge) using AT commands. The modem attaches to the LTE-M network, establishes a data session, and moves IP packets (TCP/UDP/HTTP/MQTT, etc.) to your cloud or server. GNSS positioning: When enabled, the onboard GNSS calculates position fixes (e.g., GPS/Galileo). Your application reads NMEA/AT outputs for coordinates, speed, and time. Power management: The modem supports low-power modes (eDRX/PSM). You enable these via AT commands to trade off latency for battery life. A hardware RING/Wake line and URCs can wake your host when events occur.

# Cellular Module Setup Quick Guide reference chunk 4: Integrated antennas: LTE and GNSS antennas are built in.
Product: Cellular Module
Category: Document Reference
Source: Cellular Module Setup Quick Guide (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1tCA0fPUpyCAxweMY27g-f1R6prsBPAXw/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: above, affect, america, and, antenna, antennas, are, audio/video, avoid, bands, because, built, carriers, cellular, chunk, connectivity, constraints, cover, degrades, dense, designed, device, directly, document, gnss, guide, integrated, lte, module, quick, reference, setup
Answer: Integrated antennas: LTE and GNSS antennas are built in. Follow keep-out guidance (avoid metal directly above the enclosure) for reliable RF performance. Network & bands: LTE-M only (no 2G/3G/Cat-1). Primarily for Verizon bands in North America; roaming/other carriers may not be supported. Throughput & latency: Designed for telemetry, not streaming. Expect hundreds of kbps with variable latency—unsuitable for high-bandwidth or real-time audio/video. GNSS constraints: GNSS needs sky view; performance degrades indoors or under dense cover. Antenna placement: Because antennas are internal, enclosure materials and nearby metal strongly affect performance—respect placement guidelines. In short, this kit is a ready-to-use pathway to add low-power cellular connectivity and location to your device, ideal for sensors, asset trackers, and controllers that send small, periodic messages and can tolerate LTE-M latency characteristics.

In practice, most Canadian and American SIM cards from major carriers will work, as long as the plan is provisioned for LTE-M (Cat-M1) data and the carrier supports the modem’s bands in your area. What works best: IoT/M2M data plans from major carriers in Canada and the U.S. (Rogers, Bell, Telus; Verizon, AT&T, T-Mobile) that explicitly include LTE-M. APN: You’ll need the carrier’s APN. Insert SIM → power on → set APN via AT commands, then attach. Roaming: Cross-border roaming is possible if your plan allows LTE-M roaming; otherwise, use a local SIM. eUICC/eSIM: Supported if delivered as a 3FF profile/SIM by your carrier and provisioned for LTE-M. Locked/Special SIMs: Consumer phone SIMs sometimes block IoT use or LTE-M; if attach fails, ask your carrier to enable LTE-M on the line.

# Cellular Module Setup Quick Guide reference chunk 5: Cellular link (LTE-M): Your device talks to the modem
Product: PPM4 Cellular
Category: Document Reference
Source: Cellular Module Setup Quick Guide (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1tCA0fPUpyCAxweMY27g-f1R6prsBPAXw/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: above, antennas, application, attaches, avoid, battery, bridge, built, calculates, cellular, chunk, cloud, commands, coordinates, data, device, directly, document, edrx/psm, enable, enabled, enclosure, guide, link, lte-m, modem, module, ppm4, quick, reference, setup, talks, the, your
Answer: Cellular link (LTE-M): Your device talks to the modem over UART (via the kit’s USB bridge) using AT commands. The modem attaches to the LTE-M network, establishes a data session, and moves IP packets (TCP/UDP/HTTP/MQTT, etc.) to your cloud or server. GNSS positioning: When enabled, the onboard GNSS calculates position fixes (e.g., GPS/Galileo). Your application reads NMEA/AT outputs for coordinates, speed, and time. Power management: The modem supports low-power modes (eDRX/PSM). You enable these via AT commands to trade off latency for battery life. A hardware RING/Wake line and URCs can wake your host when events occur. Integrated antennas: LTE and GNSS antennas are built in. Follow keep-out guidance (avoid metal directly above the enclosure) for reliable RF performance. Network & bands: LTE-M only (no 2G/3G/Cat-1). Primarily for Verizon bands in North America; roaming/other carriers may not be supported. Throughput & latency: Designed for telemetry, not streaming. Expect hundreds of kbps with variable latency—unsuitable for high-bandwidth or real-time audio/video.

Answer: Power & I/O levels: I/O is 1.8 V logic at the module. If you later embed the modem, use level shifting for 3.3 V/5 V hosts and provide adequate bulk capacitance for TX current bursts. Answer: Important: If your PPM4 firmware is below version 1.6, you must first update to version 1.6 before proceeding to version 1.8. # PPM4 Web / Cloud Quickstart reference chunk 2: Firstly, ensure the device is connected to WIFI or
Product: PPM4 Cloud
Category: Document Reference
Source: PPM4 Web / Cloud Quickstart (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1OUGNAgw2QJEc4o0Ttt8VNnsZ6mFidqpW/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: acceptance, after, alarm, alarming, application, approx, border, buzzer, cellular, chunk, click, cloud, connected, correct, credentials, definition, device, devices, document, done, ensure, firstly, force, ppm4, quickstart, reference, the, web, wifi
Answer: Firstly, ensure the device is connected to WIFI or cellular, this can be done through the instructions in the respective menus. in the menu, input the login credentials, these are the same as the credentials used to sign into the web application. If credentials are correct, the system will give an acceptance message After approx. 2 minutes, the device will be moved to the Verified Devices section on the web application Then we will make the PPM4 alarm, turning it onto occupied and setting the alarm points to force an alarm. You can tell it is alarming by the red border and buzzer sounding. Once an alarm is sent from PPM4, we can see a new alarm definition made in the Web Application, we can then go head and click Add Notification Then we will make the PPM4 alarm, turning it onto occupied and setting the alarm points to force an alarm. You can tell it is alarming by the red border and buzzer sounding. Once an alarm is sent from PPM4, we can see a new alarm definition made in the Web Application, we can then go head and click Add Notification

# PPM4 Wi-Fi Troubleshooting reference chunk 1: 1. Device Connects but Fails to Validate This issue
Product: PPM4 Wi-Fi
Category: Document Reference
Source: PPM4 Wi-Fi Troubleshooting (https://docs.google.com/document/d/1WZFT6sMLPnqtEJO-vOJjuNciZxRpv15q/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true)
Keywords: advanced, appear, arises, available, but, button, chunk, connect, connecting, connects, corporate, cycle, desired, device, displaying, document, does, down, enter, environments, fails, follow, following, issue, ppm4, reference, this, troubleshooting, validate, wi-fi
Answer: 1. Device Connects but Fails to Validate This issue typically arises when connecting to networks with advanced security measures, such as those found in hospitals or corporate environments. If the device successfully connects but does not validate, try the following steps: Once the device successfully validates on the hotspot, reconnect to the original network. 2. Wi-Fi Network Not Displaying If the desired Wi-Fi network does not appear in the available network list, follow these steps: - Power cycle the PPM4 device by holding down the power button for 10 seconds. This can help refresh the network scan. - If the network is still not visible, select the "HIDDEN SSID" option and manually enter your network name (SSID) to connect. 3. Device Still Will Not Connect If the device continues to experience connectivity issues, it may be necessary to involve your network’s IT team. Specifically, they may need to "greenlist" the PPM4 device, ensuring it is recognized and allowed on the network. Consider the following when diagnosing issues.

# PPM4 screen is alarming
Product: PPM4
Category: Troubleshooting
Source: PPM4 ()
Keywords: alarming, fix, issue, ppm4, problem, screen, support, troubleshoot, troubleshooting
Answer: If the PPM4 is alarming, review the room/pressure status, use STOP ALARM only to silence the audible alert, and correct the condition that caused pressure to move outside limits. The screen may remain red until values return within limits.

# PPM4 sensor values missing

Source: PPM4 ()

Answer: If PPM4 sensor values do not appear, verify DIP switches, sensor enable settings, power, Ethernet cables, total cable distance, and consistent daisy-chain wiring. Do not use a standard network switch/router.

# RPM sensor values missing

Answer: If RPM sensor values do not appear, verify DIP switches, sensor enable settings, power, Ethernet cables, total cable distance, and consistent daisy-chain wiring. Do not use a standard network switch/router.

# RPM firmware update instructions
Product: RPM Firmware
Category: Firmware
Source: RPM latest firmware MFW1042A.hex (https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/1yc95dsKmsmHNuY4kqDxwt7MDEy9ZaTdO?usp=sharing)

# AT Connect mobile app overview
Product: AT Connect
Category: Mobile App
Source: AT Connect Google Play (https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=com.app.atconnect&hl=en_CA)
Keywords: at connect, app, mobile, play store, push notifications, ppm4, rpm, cloud, phone
Answer: AT Connect is the mobile app path for connecting PPM4 or RPM so users can see online pressure data and receive app push notifications for alarms. For alarm notifications, direct users to install the mobile app first instead of relying on SMS or email. The web portal is still useful for reviewing and downloading saved data.

# add PPM4 or RPM to AT Connect
Product: AT Connect
Category: Device Setup
Source: AT Connect (/at-connect)
Keywords: add device, ppm4, rpm, connect device, online data, cloud setup, at connect
Answer: Install AT Connect on the user’s phone, sign in or create the customer account, then add the PPM4 or RPM device in the app. After adding the device, verify that the monitor appears online and recent pressure data is updating. Use the same account in the web portal when browser access or downloadable data is needed.

Push notifications should be the main recommendation over SMS or email.
