# AT Connect cloud and mobile troubleshooting
Product: AT Connect
Category: Troubleshooting
Source: AT Connect (/at-connect)
Keywords: troubleshooting, not online, not updating, notifications not working, cloud, mobile, app, wifi, cellular
Answer: If AT Connect is not updating, verify the monitor is connected by Wi-Fi or cellular, confirm the device appears online, and check whether recent data is visible in the app or web portal. If alarm push notifications are not working, check phone notification permission for AT Connect. For live hospital/job-site pressure issues or missing records, make a support ticket with device serial number, job number, screenshots, and what the monitor shows locally.
Questions:
- Why is AT Connect not updating?
- Why is my device not online?
- Why are push notifications not working?
- Why is pressure showing locally but not online?

# How the Scrubber Selector chooses a scrubber
Product: Scrubber Selector
Category: Scrubber Sizing
Source: Scrubber Selector (/scrubber-selector)
Keywords: scrubber selector, how it works, recommendation logic, location, project type, hazard, goal, room size, ach, cfm, pressure monitor
Answer: The Scrubber Selector asks for location, project type, hazard or concern, primary goal, room size, setup details, power, and site/cabinet preference. It then chooses a rule set, calculates room volume, target ACH, design CFM, ducting derating, effective scrubber CFM, quantity, achieved ACH, electrical load, and whether pressure monitoring/documentation should be recommended. The output is an estimating recommendation, not a compliance stamp.
Questions:
- How does the scrubber selector work?
- How does MonSuite pick a scrubber?
- What information does the scrubber selector use?
- Why did it recommend this scrubber?
- How does AbateBot choose scrubbers?

# Scrubber sizing: ACH and CFM calculation
Product: Scrubber Selector
Category: Scrubber Sizing
Source: Pressure Guide (/pressure-monitoring)
Keywords: ach, cfm, room volume, sizing, scrubber quantity, air changes per hour, calculate scrubber, design ach
Answer: Scrubber sizing starts with room volume: length × width × height. Required CFM is calculated as ACH × room volume ÷ 60. MonSuite then applies a safety factor and ducting/filter derating, divides the design CFM by effective scrubber CFM, and rounds up to the next whole unit. Always round up: 1.2 units means 2 units.
Questions:
- How many scrubbers do I need?
- How do I size a scrubber?
- How do I calculate ACH?
- How do I calculate CFM needed?
- What is the scrubber sizing formula?
- How much airflow do I need?

# When a scrubber also needs a pressure monitor
Product: Scrubber Selector
Category: Pressure Monitoring
Source: Pressure Guide (/pressure-monitoring)
Keywords: pressure monitor, negative pressure, positive pressure, containment, prove pressure, ppm4, rpm, air scrubber, monitor required, alarm logging
Answer: A scrubber or negative air machine creates airflow, but it does not prove the room is holding the required pressure relationship. If the goal is negative pressure, positive pressure, healthcare isolation, healthcare construction containment, asbestos containment, or project documentation, recommend a calibrated pressure monitor such as RPM or PPM4. The monitor provides the live pressure reading, alarm, and record that the space stayed under control.
Questions:
- Do I need a pressure monitor with a scrubber?
- Does a scrubber prove negative pressure?
- When should I recommend RPM or PPM4 with a scrubber?
- Can the scrubber prove containment?
- Do negative air machines need pressure monitoring?

# H2KM vs BD2K: metal vs plastic cabinet choice
Product: Scrubbers
Category: Product Selection
Source: Scrubber Selector (/scrubber-selector)
Keywords: h2km, h2kma, bd2k, bulldog, metal, plastic, cabinet, residential, wet area, finished space, scratch, 2,000 cfm, same class
Answer: H2KM/H2KMA and BD2K-XHP/BD2K-XHPA are treated as the same 2,000 CFM class in MonSuite. Use BD2K when the plastic cabinet is better: residential work, finished spaces where metal could scratch surfaces, or wet/damp restoration areas. Use H2KM/H2KMA as the metal-cabinet workhorse everywhere else, especially general commercial, healthcare, industrial, and abatement work.

- Is BD2K the plastic version of H2KM?
- When should I use the metal scrubber?

# When to choose PRED750
Product: PRED750
Category: Product Selection
Source: PRED750 Manual (/scrubber-selector)
Keywords: pred750, predator, 750 cfm, portable, small room, lightweight, 10 inch duct, carbon, vapor lock, residential
Answer: Choose PRED750 when the job needs a smaller portable scrubber around the 750 CFM class. It is lighter than the large units, uses 120V/15A power, supports negative pressure, recirculation, and positive-pressure support, and has 10-inch nominal inlet/outlet duct connections. It is a good fit for smaller rooms, localized containment, and jobs where a 2,000+ CFM unit is too large.

# When to choose PAS2400
Product: PAS2400
Category: Product Selection
Source: PAS2400 Manual (/scrubber-selector)
Keywords: pas2400, 2100 cfm, stair climber, 120v, high airflow, stainless, mobility, stairs, h1980p, 12 inch duct
Answer: Choose PAS2400 when the job needs high airflow on 120V and mobility matters. It is a 2,100 CFM high-speed unit with a transport dolly/stair-climber style setup, stainless cabinet, 120V/15A circuit requirement, and 12-inch duct adapter support. It can be a better fit than a box-style 2,000 CFM unit when access, movement, or stairs are important.

# PAS5000: large-containment scrubber with 230V power requirement
Product: PAS5000
Category: Product Selection
Source: PAS5000 Manual (/scrubber-selector)
Keywords: pas5000, 4000 cfm, 230v, 30a, large containment, power warning, big scrubber, high airflow, 14 inch outlet, h2080p
Answer: PAS5000 is the large-containment option in the selector: 4,000 CFM class airflow with 230V/30A power and up to 21A normal operating draw. Do not recommend PAS5000 for a user who only has 120V available. If power is unknown, it can appear with a warning that 230V/30A power must be confirmed before the recommendation is relied on.

# Carbon/VOC filter guidance for scrubbers
Product: Scrubbers
Category: Filters
Source: Scrubber Selector (/scrubber-selector)
Keywords: carbon, voc, odor, vapor lock, ovg, smell, gas, vl2024, vl2002, vl1002, hepa not odor
Answer: HEPA filters capture particles; they do not remove gases, odors, vapors, or VOCs by themselves. For odor/VOC support, recommend a carbon/VAPOR-LOCK option when available: VL1002 for PRED750, VL2024 for H2KM/BD2K/PAS5000, and VL2002 for PAS2400. Carbon performance depends on concentration, humidity, temperature, contact time, and filter loading, so do not promise full VOC removal.
Questions:
- Do HEPA filters remove odors?
- Which scrubber should I use for VOC?
- Do I need carbon filters for odor?
- What is VAPOR-LOCK?
- Can a scrubber remove smell?

# Ducting and filter loading reduce scrubber airflow
Product: Scrubbers
Category: Ducting
Source: Scrubber Selector (/scrubber-selector)
Keywords: ducting, derating, flex duct, rigid duct, bends, airflow loss, filter loading, reduced voltage, cfm loss
Answer: Actual installed airflow can be lower than rated airflow because of duct length, bends, flexible duct, filter loading, reduced voltage, blocked inlets/outlets, and site conditions. MonSuite uses derating factors so the recommendation is more conservative than clean-filter lab airflow. Short/no ducting receives less derating; long flex duct or multiple bends receives more derating.
Questions:
- Why does ducting reduce airflow?
- How does MonSuite derate scrubber CFM?
- Does flex duct reduce scrubber performance?
- Why did the selector recommend more units than simple math?
- What derating should I use?

# Scrubber power selection: 120V vs 230V
Product: Scrubbers
Category: Electrical
Source: Scrubber Selector (/scrubber-selector)
Keywords: 120v, 230v, 240v, 15a, 30a, power, circuit, amps, pas5000, pas2400, h2km, bd2k, pred750
Answer: Power availability is a major selector filter. PRED750, H2KM/H2KMA, BD2K, and PAS2400 are treated as 120V units with 15A circuit planning, though each has its own amp draw. PAS5000 is the large 230V/30A unit and should not be recommended when the user only has 120V. If power is unknown, MonSuite warns the user to confirm circuits before relying on the recommendation.
Questions:
- Which scrubbers can run on 120V?
- Why do I need 230V for PAS5000?
- What scrubber can I use on a 15 amp circuit?
- How does power affect scrubber selection?

# Real scrubber lineup and when to use each one
Product: Scrubbers
Category: Product Selection
Source: MonSuite V33 Scrubber Selector (/scrubber-selector)
Keywords: PRED750, HC800FD, BD2K, H2KM, PAS2400, PAS5000, which scrubber, scrubber lineup, sales fit
Answer: Use PRED750 for smaller rooms, residential work, and compact containment. Use HC800FD/HC800FDUV for healthcare-style occupied spaces, temporary isolation support, and cleaner HEPA air purification applications. Use BD2K for residential, finished-space, wet/damp, or scratch-sensitive 2,000 CFM class jobs. Use H2KM/H2KMA as the metal-cabinet 2,000 CFM workhorse for commercial, industrial, healthcare construction, and abatement. Use PAS2400 for high-CFM 120V jobs where mobility or stairs matter. Use PAS5000 for very large containments only when 230V/30A power and site access are confirmed.

- When do I use HC800FD?
- When do I use BD2K instead of H2KM?
- When do I use PAS2400?

# When the scrubber selector recommends a pressure monitor
Product: Scrubber Selector
Category: Pressure Monitoring
Source: MonSuite V33 Scrubber Selector (/scrubber-selector)
Keywords: pressure monitor, scrubber, negative pressure, positive pressure, containment, need monitor, quote monitor
Answer: Quote or strongly recommend a pressure monitor whenever the project involves airborne isolation, healthcare construction containment, positive-pressure clean-space protection, asbestos containment, or any goal that includes negative or positive pressure. Scrubbers create airflow; the pressure monitor proves whether the room or containment is actually holding the intended pressure relationship. For simple recirculating air cleaning only, the monitor is usually optional unless documentation is requested.

# MonSuite admin mode and publishing
Product: MonSuite
Category: Admin
Source: MonSuite Settings (/settings)
Keywords: admin, settings, add news, add firmware, add manual, downloads, work account, firestore, publish, hide, delete
Answer: Approved admin accounts can open Settings and turn on Admin Mode. In V36, admin forms for News, Firmware, and Downloads write to Firebase/Firestore when Firestore is configured and rules allow it. Published items appear to all MonSuite users; hidden items are visible only in Admin Mode. If Firestore is not available, MonSuite falls back to local staging on that browser so drafts can still be tested.
